<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.

* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:

</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

1.1 Problem Description Analysis:
- The bug is in the `from_json` function not correctly converting BulkDataURI's in SQ (Sequence) data elements
- When DICOM objects with large data elements in SQ elements are converted to JSON, they become BulkDataURI's correctly
- But when converting back from JSON to DICOM using `from_json`, the BulkDataURI's in SQ data elements are not converted back properly
- Warnings are thrown during this process

1.2 Technical Details:
- File mentioned: `jsonrep.py` at line 227
- Test data file: `waveform_ecg.dcm`
- Method involved: `from_json`
- Data types involved: BulkDataURI's in SQ (Sequence) data elements

1.3 Clear Problem Statement:
The issue is that the `from_json` function in pydicom handles BulkDataURI conversion correctly for regular data elements but fails to handle BulkDataURI conversion for data elements that are inside Sequence (SQ) elements.

1.4 Steps to Reproduce:
1. Take the `waveform_ecg.dcm` test data file
2. Convert it to JSON format
3. Convert the JSON back to DICOM using `from_json`
4. Observe that BulkDataURI's in SQ elements are not converted back correctly and warnings are generated

     8	# *pydicom*
     9	
    10	*pydicom* is a pure Python package for working with [DICOM](https://www.dicomstandard.org/) files. It lets you read, modify and write DICOM data in an easy "pythonic" way.
    11	
    12	As a pure Python package, *pydicom* can run anywhere Python runs without any other requirements, although if you're working with *Pixel Data* then we recommend you also install [NumPy](http://www.numpy.org).
    13	
    14	If you're looking for a Python library for DICOM networking then you might be interested in another of our projects: [pynetdicom](https://github.com/pydicom/pynetdicom).
    15	
    16	## Installation
    17	
    18	Using [pip](https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/):
    19	```
    20	pip install pydicom
    21	```
    22	Using [conda](https://docs.conda.io/en/latest/):
    23	```
    24	conda install -c conda-forge pydicom
    25	```
    26	
    27	For more information, including installation instructions for the development version, see the [installation guide](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable/tutorials/installation.html).
    28	
    29	
    30	## Documentation
    31	
    32	The *pydicom* [user guide](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable/old/pydicom_user_guide.html), [tutorials](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable/tutorials/index.html), [examples](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable/auto_examples/index.html) and [API reference](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable/reference/index.html) documentation is available for both the [current release](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable) and the [development version](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/dev) on GitHub Pages.
    33	
    34	## *Pixel Data*
    35	
    36	Compressed and uncompressed *Pixel Data* is always available to
    37	be read, changed and written as [bytes](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#bytes-objects):
    38	```python
    39	>>> from pydicom import dcmread
    40	>>> from pydicom.data import get_testdata_file
    41	>>> path = get_testdata_file("CT_small.dcm")
    42	>>> ds = dcmread(path)
    43	>>> type(ds.PixelData)
    44	<class 'bytes'>
    45	>>> len(ds.PixelData)
    46	32768
    47	>>> ds.PixelData[:2]
    48	b'\xaf\x00'
    49	
    50	```
    51	
    52	If [NumPy](http://www.numpy.org) is installed, *Pixel Data* can be converted to an [ndarray](https://numpy.org/doc/stable/reference/generated/numpy.ndarray.html) using the [Dataset.pixel_array](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable/reference/generated/pydicom.dataset.Dataset.html#pydicom.dataset.Dataset.pixel_array) property:
    53	
    54	```python
    55	>>> arr = ds.pixel_array
    56	>>> arr.shape
    57	(128, 128)
    58	>>> arr
    59	array([[175, 180, 166, ..., 203, 207, 216],
    60	       [186, 183, 157, ..., 181, 190, 239],
    61	       [184, 180, 171, ..., 152, 164, 235],
    62	       ...,
    63	       [906, 910, 923, ..., 922, 929, 927],
    64	       [914, 954, 938, ..., 942, 925, 905],
    65	       [959, 955, 916, ..., 911, 904, 909]], dtype=int16)
    66	```
    67	### Compressed *Pixel Data*
    68	#### JPEG, JPEG-LS and JPEG 2000
    69	Converting JPEG compressed *Pixel Data* to an ``ndarray`` requires installing one or more additional Python libraries. For information on which libraries are required, see the [pixel data handler documentation](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/dev/old/image_data_handlers.html#guide-compressed).
    70	
    71	Compressing data into one of the JPEG formats is not currently supported.
    72	
    73	#### RLE
    74	RLE encoded *Pixel Data* only requires NumPy, and compression and decompression are both supported.
    75	
    76	## Examples
    77	More [examples](https://pydicom.github.io/pydicom/stable/auto_examples/index.html) are available in the documentation.
    78	
    79	**Change a patient's ID**
    80	```python
    81	from pydicom import dcmread
    82	
    83	ds = dcmread("/path/to/file.dcm")
    84	# Edit the (0010,0020) 'Patient ID' element
    85	ds.PatientID = "12345678"
    86	ds.save_as("/path/to/file_updated.dcm")
    87	```
    88	
    89	**Display the Pixel Data**
    90	
    91	With [NumPy](http://www.numpy.org) and [matplotlib](https://matplotlib.org/)
    92	```python
    93	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    94	from pydicom import dcmread
    95	from pydicom.data import get_testdata_file
    96	
    97	# The path to a pydicom test dataset
    98	path = get_testdata_file("CT_small.dcm")
    99	ds = dcmread(path)
   100	# `arr` is a numpy.ndarray
   101	arr = ds.pixel_array
   102	
   103	plt.imshow(arr, cmap="gray")
   104	plt.show()
   105	```
   106	
   107	## Contributing
   108	
   109	To contribute to *pydicom*, read our [contribution guide](https://github.com/pydicom/pydicom/blob/master/CONTRIBUTING.md).
   110	
   111	To contribute an example or extension of *pydicom* that doesn't belong with the core software, see our contribution repository:
   112	[contrib-pydicom](https://www.github.com/pydicom/contrib-pydicom).

1. The `JsonDataElementConverter` class stores the `bulk_data_uri_handler` in its constructor (line 108)
2. When processing sequences, the `get_sequence_item` method is called (line 175)
3. Inside `get_sequence_item`, when it encounters data elements within the sequence, it calls `DataElement.from_json()` (lines 227-230)

This means that when processing BulkDataURI elements inside sequences, there's no handler available, so warnings are generated and the elements aren't converted properly.

These should be converted to BulkDataURI when converting to JSON with bulk data URI enabled.

Let me create a reproduction script:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pydicom__pydicom__2.1/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Reproduction script for BulkDataURI issue in SQ elements.
"""

import warnings
import pydicom
from pydicom.data import get_testdata_file
from pydicom import dcmread


def test_bulkdatauri_in_sq():
    """Test the issue with BulkDataURI conversion in SQ elements."""
    
    # Load the test data
    filepath = get_testdata_file('waveform_ecg.dcm')
    ds = dcmread(filepath)
    
    print("=== Original Dataset ===")
    print(f"Dataset loaded successfully: {ds}")
    
    # Find sequences with large binary data
    waveforms_found = []
    for elem in ds:
        if elem.VR == 'SQ' and elem.keyword == 'WaveformSequence':
            print(f"Found WaveformSequence with {len(elem.value)} items")
            for i, item in enumerate(elem.value):
                for sub_elem in item:
                    if sub_elem.keyword == 'WaveformData' and len(sub_elem.value) > 1000:
                        waveforms_found.append((i, sub_elem.tag, len(sub_elem.value)))
                        print(f"  Item {i}: WaveformData length = {len(sub_elem.value)} bytes")
    
    # Convert to JSON with bulk data URI
    print("\n=== Converting to JSON with BulkDataURI ===")
    
    def bulk_data_handler(value):
        """Simple bulk data handler that returns placeholder data."""
        return b"placeholder_data"
    
    json_string = ds.to_json(bulk_data_uri_handler=bulk_data_handler)
    print("JSON conversion successful")
    
    # Print a portion of the JSON to see BulkDataURI elements
    print("Sample of JSON (first 1000 chars):")
    print(json_string[:1000])
    
    # Now convert back from JSON - this should trigger the issue
    print("\n=== Converting back from JSON ===")
    
    # Capture warnings
    with warnings.catch_warnings(record=True) as w:
        warnings.simplefilter("always")
        
        # Convert back from JSON
        ds_restored = pydicom.dataset.Dataset.from_json(json_string)
        
        # Check if any warnings were generated
        bulkdata_warnings = [warning for warning in w 
                           if "bulk data URI handler" in str(warning.message)]
        
        print(f"Number of bulk data URI warnings: {len(bulkdata_warnings)}")
        for warning in bulkdata_warnings:
            print(f"Warning: {warning.message}")
            
    # Check if the WaveformData elements were properly restored
    print("\n=== Checking restored dataset ===")
    restored_waveforms = []
    for elem in ds_restored:
        if elem.VR == 'SQ' and elem.keyword == 'WaveformSequence':
            print(f"Restored WaveformSequence with {len(elem.value)} items")
            for i, item in enumerate(elem.value):
                for sub_elem in item:
                    if sub_elem.keyword == 'WaveformData':
                        restored_waveforms.append((i, sub_elem.tag, 
                                                 len(sub_elem.value) if hasattr(sub_elem.value, '__len__') else 0))
                        print(f"  Item {i}: WaveformData length = {len(sub_elem.value) if hasattr(sub_elem.value, '__len__') else 'N/A'}")
                        
    # Compare original and restored
    print(f"\nOriginal large waveforms found: {len(waveforms_found)}")
    print(f"Restored waveforms found: {len(restored_waveforms)}")
    
    if len(bulkdata_warnings) > 0:
        print("\n❌ ISSUE CONFIRMED: BulkDataURI warnings were generated!")
        print("This indicates that BulkDataURI elements in SQ are not handled correctly.")
        return False
    else:
        print("\n✅ No warnings - BulkDataURI elements handled correctly!")
        return True


if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = test_bulkdatauri_in_sq()
    exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/pydicom__pydicom__2.1/reproduce_issue.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/pydicom__pydicom__2.1 && python reproduce_issue.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>=== Original Dataset ===

Help on function to_json in module pydicom.dataset:

to_json(self, bulk_data_threshold: int = 1024, bulk_data_element_handler: Optional[Callable[[pydicom.dataelem.DataElement], str]] = None, dump_handler: Optional[Callable[[ForwardRef('Dataset')], str]] = None) -> str
    Return a JSON representation of the :class:`Dataset`.

    .. versionadded:: 1.3

    See the DICOM Standard, Part 18, :dcm:`Annex F<part18/chapter_F.html>`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bulk_data_threshold : int, optional
        Threshold for the length of a base64-encoded binary data element
        above which the element should be considered bulk data and the
        value provided as a URI rather than included inline (default:
        ``1024``). Ignored if no bulk data handler is given.
    bulk_data_element_handler : callable, optional
        Callable function that accepts a bulk data element and returns a
        JSON representation of the data element (dictionary including the
        "vr" key and either the "InlineBinary" or the "BulkDataURI" key).
    dump_handler : callable, optional
        Callable function that accepts a :class:`dict` and returns the
        serialized (dumped) JSON string (by default uses
        :func:`json.dumps`).

    def test_valid_bulkdata_uri(self):
        ds_json = ('{"00091002": {"vr": "OB", "BulkDataURI": '
                   '"http://example.com/bulkdatahandler"}}')
        msg = r"no bulk data URI handler provided"
        with pytest.warns(UserWarning, match=msg):
            ds = Dataset.from_json(ds_json)
        assert 0x00091002 in ds

        ds_json = ('{"00091002": {"vr": "OB", "BulkDataURI": '
                   '["http://example.com/bulkdatahandler"]}}')
        with pytest.warns(UserWarning, match=msg):
            ds = Dataset.from_json(ds_json)
        assert 0x00091002 in ds

    def test_invalid_bulkdata_uri(self):
        msg = ('"BulkDataURI" of data element "00091002" '
               'must be a string.')
        ds_json = '{"00091002": {"vr": "OB", "BulkDataURI": 42}}'
        with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=msg):
            Dataset.from_json(ds_json)

        ds_json = '{"00091002": {"vr": "OB", "BulkDataURI": [42]}}'
        with pytest.raises(TypeError, match=msg):
            Dataset.from_json(ds_json)

    def test_bulk_data_reader_is_called(self):
        def bulk_data_reader(value):
            return b'xyzzy'

        json_data = {
            "00091002": {"vr": "OB", "BulkDataURI": "https://a.dummy.url"}
        }
        ds = Dataset().from_json(json.dumps(json_data), bulk_data_reader)

        assert b'xyzzy' == ds[0x00091002].value

    def test_bulk_data_reader_is_called_2(self):
        def bulk_data_reader(tag, vr, value):
            return b'xyzzy'

            "00091002": {"vr": "OB", "BulkDataURI": "https://a.dummy.url"}
        }
        ds = Dataset().from_json(json.dumps(json_data), bulk_data_reader)

    Return a JSON representation of the :class:`Dataset`.

    .. versionadded:: 1.3

    See the DICOM Standard, Part 18, :dcm:`Annex F<part18/chapter_F.html>`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    bulk_data_threshold : int, optional
        Threshold for the length of a base64-encoded binary data element
        above which the element should be considered bulk data and the
        value provided as a URI rather than included inline (default:
        ``1024``). Ignored if no bulk data handler is given.
    bulk_data_element_handler : callable, optional
        Callable function that accepts a bulk data element and returns a
        JSON representation of the data element (dictionary including the
        "vr" key and either the "InlineBinary" or the "BulkDataURI" key).
    dump_handler : callable, optional
        Callable function that accepts a :class:`dict` and returns the
        serialized (dumped) JSON string (by default uses
        :func:`json.dumps`).

        .. note:

            Make sure to use a dump handler that sorts the keys (see
            example below) to create DICOM-conformant JSON.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        :class:`Dataset` serialized into a string based on the DICOM JSON
        Model.

    Examples

Let me look at the issue differently and create a simpler reproduction script:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pydicom__pydicom__2.1/simple_repro.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Simple reproduction script for BulkDataURI issue in SQ elements.
"""

import warnings
import pydicom
from pydicom.data import get_testdata_file
from pydicom import dcmread
import json


def test_bulkdatauri_issue():
    """Test the issue with BulkDataURI conversion in SQ elements."""
    
    # Load the test data
    filepath = get_testdata_file('waveform_ecg.dcm')
    ds = dcmread(filepath)
    
    print("=== Original Dataset ===")
    
    # Find sequences with large binary data
    waveforms_found = []
    for elem in ds:
        if elem.VR == 'SQ' and elem.keyword == 'WaveformSequence':
            print(f"Found WaveformSequence with {len(elem.value)} items")
            for i, item in enumerate(elem.value):
                for sub_elem in item:
                    if sub_elem.keyword == 'WaveformData' and len(sub_elem.value) > 1000:
                        waveforms_found.append((i, sub_elem.tag, len(sub_elem.value)))
                        print(f"  Item {i}: WaveformData length = {len(sub_elem.value)} bytes")
    
    # Convert to JSON with bulk data URI
    print("\n=== Converting to JSON with BulkDataURI ===")
    
    def bulk_data_handler(element):
        """Simple bulk data handler that returns proper JSON structure."""
        return {
            'vr': element.VR,
            'BulkDataURI': f"http://example.com/bulkdata/{element.tag}"
        }
    
    json_string = ds.to_json(
        bulk_data_threshold=1000,  # Elements larger than 1000 bytes become BulkDataURI
        bulk_data_element_handler=bulk_data_handler
    )
    print("JSON conversion successful")
    
    # Parse the JSON to examine structure
    json_data = json.loads(json_string)
    
    # Look for BulkDataURI elements
    def find_bulkdata_uris(obj, path=""):
        if isinstance(obj, dict):
            if 'BulkDataURI' in obj and 'vr' in obj:
                print(f"BulkDataURI found at {path}: {obj}")
            for key, value in obj.items():
                find_bulkdata_uris(value, f"{path}.{key}" if path else key)
        elif isinstance(obj, list):
            for i, item in enumerate(obj):
                find_bulkdata_uris(item, f"{path}[{i}]")
    
    print("\n=== Examining JSON structure for BulkDataURI elements ===")
    find_bulkdata_uris(json_data)
    
    # Now convert back from JSON - this should trigger the issue
    print("\n=== Converting back from JSON ===")
    
    # Capture warnings
    with warnings.catch_warnings(record=True) as w:
        warnings.simplefilter("always")
        
        try:
            # Convert back from JSON
            ds_restored = pydicom.dataset.Dataset.from_json(json_string)
            print("Conversion successful!")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Conversion failed with error: {e}")
            return False
            
        # Check if any warnings were generated
        bulkdata_warnings = [warning for warning in w 
                           if "bulk data URI handler" in str(warning.message)]
        
        print(f"Number of bulk data URI warnings: {len(bulkdata_warnings)}")
        for warning in bulkdata_warnings:
            print(f"Warning: {warning.message}")
            
    return len(bulkdata_warnings) == 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = test_bulkdatauri_issue()
    print(f"\n{'✅ SUCCESS' if success else '❌ FAILED'}: Issue {'not reproduced' if success else 'reproduced'}")
    exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/pydicom__pydicom__2.1/simple_repro.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/pydicom__pydicom__2.1 && python simple_repro.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>=== Original Dataset ===

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I have a clear understanding of the issue:

5.1 **Problem Statement**: 
When converting DICOM to JSON, large binary data elements in sequences (SQ) are correctly converted to BulkDataURI format. However, when converting back from JSON to DICOM, the BulkDataURI elements within sequences are not processed correctly because the bulk_data_uri_handler is not passed down to the recursive DataElement.from_json() calls.

5.2 **Location of Problem**: 
In `/workspace/pydicom__pydicom__2.1/pydicom/jsonrep.py` at line 227-230 in the `get_sequence_item` method. The call to `DataElement.from_json()` does not pass the `bulk_data_uri_handler`.

5.3 **How the Test Reproduces the Issue**:
- Load waveform_ecg.dcm which contains large binary data in sequences
- Convert to JSON with bulk_data_element_handler - this works correctly
- But the JSON structure gets malformed due to incorrect recursion
- When converting back from JSON, the malformed structure causes a TypeError

5.4 **Best Practices for the Fix**:
- Pass the bulk_data_uri_handler from the current JsonDataElementConverter instance to recursive calls
- Ensure the fix doesn't break existing functionality
- Make minimal changes to fix the specific issue

5.5 **How to Fix the Problem**:
In the `get_sequence_item` method, when calling `DataElement.from_json()`, I need to pass the `bulk_data_uri_handler` from the current instance (`self.bulk_data_element_handler`) as the `bulk_data_uri_handler` parameter.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 6. """
Debug the to_json process to understand where the nesting issue occurs.
"""

import pydicom
from pydicom.data import get_testdata_file
from pydicom import dcmread


def debug_bulk_data_handler(element):
    """Debug bulk data handler that prints what it receives and returns."""
    print(f"Handler called for element {element.tag} with VR {element.VR}, length {len(element.value) if hasattr(element.value, '__len__') else 'N/A'}")
    result = {
        'vr': element.VR,
        'BulkDataURI': f"http://example.com/bulkdata/{element.tag}"
    }
    print(f"Handler returning: {result}")
    return result


def test_tojson_process():
    """Test the to_json process to see where nesting occurs."""
    
    # Load the test data
    filepath = get_testdata_file('waveform_ecg.dcm')
    ds = dcmread(filepath)
    
    print("=== Converting to JSON ===")
    
    json_string = ds.to_json(
        bulk_data_threshold=1000,
        bulk_data_element_handler=debug_bulk_data_handler
    )
    
    print("\n=== JSON Result (first 1000 chars) ===")
    print(json_string[:1000])
    
    # Save full JSON for inspection
    with open('/tmp/debug_tojson.json', 'w') as f:
        f.write(json_string)
    print("\nFull JSON saved to /tmp/debug_tojson.json")

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Now I can clearly see the issue. The JSON structure shows:

```json
"54001010": {
  "BulkDataURI": {
    "BulkDataURI": "http://example.com/bulkdata/(5400, 1010)",
    "vr": "OW"
  },
  "vr": "OW"
}
```

This means that the bulk data handler's return value (which is a dictionary with "vr" and "BulkDataURI" keys) is being treated as the value for the "BulkDataURI" key, resulting in nested structure.

The issue is not in my fix to `from_json`, but rather in how the `to_json` process works. The bulk_data_element_handler should return just the URI string, not the full dictionary. Let me check the documentation again.

Looking at the test that passes, the bulk_data_reader function in the test just returns the data (b'xyzzy'), not a dictionary. And looking at the valid_bulkdata_uri test, the JSON has:

```json
{"00091002": {"vr": "OB", "BulkDataURI": "http://example.com/bulkdatahandler"}}
```

So the bulk_data_element_handler should return just the URI string.
